hi_IN Example 1: 
User Request: 
िसर ददर् के िलए कोई घरेलू उपाय बताए ं । 
Response: अदरक की चाय पीनेसे िसरददर् में आराम िमल सकताहै। T ake care and 
get well soon. 
Example 2: 
User Request: 
िदवाली क्यों मनाई जातीहै? 
Response: िदवाली एक प्रमुख सण (Sann) है जो बुराई पर अच्छाई की जीत का प्रतीक 
है। 
Example 3: 
User Request: 
फोन की बैटरी क ै से बचाए ं? 
Response: आप अपने फोन की Settings में जाकर Battery Saver मोड ऑन कर 
सकते हैं।
Wrong 
Language
Rationale 1: The phrase "T ake care and get well 
soon" is in English. Even though Hindi speakers 
often use English phrases in casual speech, a formal 
hi_IN response must not switch languages unless 
requested. The entire response should be in Hindi 
(Devanagari). 
Rationale 2: The word "सण" is a Marathi word for 
"Festival." In Hindi, the correct word is "त्योहार" or 
"पवर्". Since "Sann" is not a valid Hindi word but 
belongs to a different language (even though it uses 
the same script), it is a Wrong Language issue. 
Rationale 3: While "Battery Saver" is a common 
term, leaving valid words like "Settings" in the Latin 
script is a localization issue. In hi_IN, commonly used 
English terms should be transliterated into 
Devanagari (e.g., सेिटंग्स, बैटरीसेवर) or translated 
(सेिटंग्स, बचत मोड). Leaving them in Latin script 
constitutes a Wrong Language issue.

(Can you tell me how the internet works?) 
Assistant Response: िबडू, टेंशन नहीं लेने का! अपुन तेरे को सब समझा देगा। इ ं टरनेट

(Buddy, don't take tension! I will explain everything to you. Internet

Example 2: 
User Request: 
Request: मुझे बैंक खाते के िलए आवेदन पत्र िलखना है।क ृ पया मेरी मदद करें。 
Assistant Response: अरे यार, यह तो बहुत आसान है। तू बस बैंक जा और फॉमर् भर दे।

slang-based persona, this sounds like a stereotype 
of a Bollywood character rather than a helpful AI 
assistant. It creates a caricature of the locale. 
Rationale 2: The user is asking for help with a formal 
task (Bank Application). The assistant responds 
using the informal pronouns तू (Tu) and तेरे (T ere), and 
casual fillers like "अरे यार" (Oh friend). In Hindi 
culture, banking and professional advice require the 
formal honorific आप (Aap). This lack of formality is a 
localization T one issue.

hi_Latn Example 1: 
User Request: 
"Mujhe handmade fashion jewelry export karne ke liye kuch tips do." 
(Translation: Give me some tips for exporting handmade fashion 
jewellery.) 
Assistant Response: 
"Bharat se handmade jewelry ka export karna ek accha vyapaar ho 
sakta hai. Aapko high-quality raw material aur unique designs par 
focus karna chahiye." 
Example 2: 
User Request: 
"Kota Factory web series ka review likho." 
(Translation: Write a review of the Kota Factory web series.) 
Assistant Response: 
"Pehla season series ke liye ek zabardast shuruaat thi, jo safalta ke 
sath dusare season aur teesare season tak pahunchi." 
Spelling Rationale 1: The assistant used the US English 
spelling "jewelry." In the hi_Latn locale, the 
standard for loanwords (English words used in 
Hindi context) follows Indian English, which 
adheres to British English conventions. The 
correct spelling in this locale is "jewellery." This 
rule applies to other common words as well (e.g., 
colour vs color, programme vs program). 
Rationale 2: The words "dusare" and "teesare" 
are non-standard transliterations of the Hindi 
words for 'second' (doosre/dusre) and 'third' 
(teesre). Writing them as "dusare" adds an 
unnecessary syllable that does not exist in the 
natural pronunciation of the word. The standard, 
phonetically accurate spellings in hi_Latn are 
"doosre" (or dusre) and "teesre." This breaks the 
natural reading flow for a native speaker.

User Request: 
"Diwali par kya pehenna chahiye?" 
(Translation: What should I wear on Diwali?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Diwali, jo Bharat ka ek pramukh tyohar hai aur jise 'Lights ka 
Festival' bhi kaha jata hai, us par aap traditional kapde jaise Kurta-
Pajama ya Saree pehen sakte hain." 
Non-local 
Perspective
Rationale 1: The assistant unnecessarily appends

A local would simply say "Mumbai se Pune..." 
Rationale 2: The assistant defines Diwali ("a major 
festival in India...") as if the user is unfamiliar with 
it. Since Diwali is a ubiquitous cultural event in the 
target locale, defining it is unnecessary and 
makes the assistant sound like an encyclopedia or 
a foreigner rather than a helpful local assistant. It 
should assume the user knows the significance of 
the festival.

Examples:
Locale Example Localization 
Issues Explanation
hi_Latn Example 1: 
User Request: 
"Kya mujhe nayi gaadi leni chahiye?" 
(Translation: Should I buy a new car?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Haan, naya gaadi lena ek accha faisla ho sakta hai kyunki purani 
gaadi mein kharcha zyada hota hai." 
Example 2: 
User Request: 
"Bache ke kamre ka rang kaisa hona chahiye?" 
(Translation: What should be the color of a child's room?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Kamra ka rang halka aur shant hona chahiye, jaise neela ya safed." 
Example 3: 
User Request: 
"Kal raat Ram ne dinner mein kya khaya?" 
(Translation: What did Ram eat for dinner last night?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Ram ne kal raat dinner mein biryani khaya." 
Grammar Rationale 1: In Hindi, the noun "gaadi" (car) is 
feminine. Therefore, the adjectives describing it 
must also be feminine. The assistant incorrectly 
used the masculine forms "naya" and "accha". 
The grammatically correct phrase in the target 
locale is "nayi gaadi" and "acchi". 
Rationale 2: This is an error in the "Oblique Case" 
(Tiryak Vibhakti). In Hindi, when a masculine noun 
ending in 'a' (like Kamra) is followed by a 
postposition (like ka), the noun must inflect/
change to end in 'e'. usage of "Kamra ka" is 
grammatically incorrect; it must be "Kamre ka". 
Rationale 3: This is a complex grammatical error 
involving the Ergative Case. In Hindi transitive 
verbs in the past tense (used with 'ne'), the verb 
must agree with the object, not the subject. Since 
"Biryani" is a feminine noun, the verb should be 
feminine, even though Ram (subject) is 
masculine.
• Incorrect: ...biryani khaya (agrees with Ram/
Subject).
• Correct: ...biryani khayi (agrees with Biryani/
Object).

hi_Latn Example 1:

"Mera laptop baar-baar hang ho raha hai, kya karoon?" 
(Translation: My laptop is hanging repeatedly, what should I do?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Yadi aapka sanganak hang ho raha hai, toh kripya use punah 
prarambh karein." 
Example 2: 
User Request: 
"Kya tum mujhe ek achha gana suggest kar sakte ho?" 
(Translation: Can you suggest a good song to me?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Haan, main aapko gana suggest karne ka kaam kar sakta hoon." 
(Literal: Yes, I can do the work of suggesting a song to you.) 
Awkward or 
unnatural 
writing
Rationale 1: While the words "sanganak" 
(computer) and "punah prarambh" (restart) are 
correct Hindi, using them in the hi_Latnlocale is 
extremely unnatural. Users writing in Roman 
script use standard English tech terms like 
"Laptop" or "Computer" and "Restart". A native 
speaker would never write "sanganak" in a chat 
context; it feels like a Sanskrit textbook forced 
into Latin script. 
Rationale 2: The phrase "suggest karne ka kaam 
kar sakta hoon" is unnecessarily wordy and 
robotic. It turns a simple verb into a complex 
"noun + support verb" structure. A native speaker 
would simply use the direct verb structure: "Haan, 
main aapko ek gana suggest kar sakta hoon." The 
added complexity makes the assistant sound 
distinctly non-human.

hi_Latn Example 3: 
User Request: 
"Exam ke liye focus kaise badhayein?" 
(Translation: How to increase focus for exams?) 
Assistant Response: 
"Exam mein focus badhane ke liye meditation karein. Yeh aapko 
shaant rehne mein madad karega aur clear your mind effectively." 
Example 4: 
User Request: "Aaj dinner mein kya banayein?" (Translation: What 
should we make for dinner today?) 
Assistant Response: "Aaj batata ki sabzi bana lete hain." 
Rationale 3: While hi_Latn allows for English 
nouns/loanwords (like 'meditation', 'focus'), 
inserting a full English clause "clear your mind 
effectively" where Hindi grammar is expected is a 
Wrong Language issue. This mimics the "lighten 
the load" example from the reference file. The 
grammar structure implies a Hindi verb should 
follow, but the assistant switched to an English 
phrase.
Correct hi_Latn: "...aur dimaag ko shaant karne 
mein madad karega." 
Rationale 4: The word "batata" is the Marathi 
word for "Potato." In standard Hindi (hi_Latn), the 
correct word is "aloo." While "batata" is widely 
understood in regions like Mumbai, it is 
technically a loanword from a different Indian 
language (Marathi/Portuguese origin) that has not 
been accepted into standard Hindi vocabulary. 
Using it constitutes a Wrong Language issue.

id_ID User Request:
Berikan rekomendasi nama restoran untuk bebek goreng. 
Assistant Response:
Berikut beberapa rekomendasi nama restoran untuk bebek goreng: 
Bebek Goreng Pak Jaya 
Warung Bebek Nusantara 
Bebek Renyah Sejahtera 
Golden Duck Kitchen 
Crispy Duck House 
Royal Duck Bistro
No Issues The assistant response is written in Indonesian, 
which matches the task locale. While some 
recommended restaurant names are in English, this 
does not constitute a Wrong Language issue. Proper 
names, brand names, and creative titles are not 
required to be in the task locale’s language and may 
reasonably appear in other languages, especially 
English, depending on branding or stylistic intent 
and the locale norms.

Content

from overseas, however the response unnecessarily 
fixates on the locale.

id_ID Request: hari ini ulang tahunku 
Assistant Response: Selamat ulang tahun! Semoga bahagia alwais. 
Grade: Issues present, Spelling
Spelling Even though the locale is id_ID, we still accept 
common loanwords (as per WL GL) and both US 
and UK English variants are acceptable.
The word has to be verifiable in official sources such 
as KBBI, LS, AD. In such cases, we will also penalize 
misspelt English words.
"Alwais" is not a valid English word.
id_ID Request: beda waktu sini dan T okyo berapa jam 
Assistant Response: Di Indonesia, kita ada 3 zona waktu, yaitu WIB, 
WITA dan WIT, dengan perbedaan masing-masing satu jam. Bedanya 
waktu dengan T okyo akan juga berbeda tergantung dengan lokasi Anda 
di Indonesia.
Non-local 
Perspective
For an Indonesian user, such additional information 
regarding local time difference is unnecessary.
id_ID Request: tolong cari di mana toilet umum terdekat 
Assistant Response: tandas terdekat ada di 1 km di hadapan Anda.
VocabularyIn id_ID locale, we have words where we share the 
same spelling with ms_MY, with similar or different 
definitions. For words that are contextually 
inappropriate or less commonly used in our locale 
but still verifiable in official sources such as KBBI, LS, 
we will consider them as vocabulary issue. 
In our locale context, we use "tandas" less 
frequently and it is more frequently used in ms_MY.
id_ID Request: tolong hitung total belanjaanku 
Assistant Response: Wah piang. Belanja lagi? Sini ku bantuin.
Phrase or 
Idiom
“wah piang” is a phrase from en_SG locale and not 
commonly used in id_ID).
id_ID Request: besok hari apa? 
Assistant Response: besok hari Isnin, 11 Ogos 2025
Wrong 
Language
Both "Isnin" and "Ogos" cannot be found in KBBI. 
These are considered Wrong Language issue 
(ms_MY).

Assistant Response: Sip. Besoke aku ndak masuk ya. Wis rampung.
Example 2:

Assistant Response: Konnichiwa! Namaku Yui-desu. Aku suka makan

id_ID Request: aku baru putus sama pacarku, tolong cariin yang baru 
Assistant Response: Engkau pasti merasa rusak sekali hatinya. T etapi 
yang kau perlukan sekarang bukanlah pacar baru tetapi waktu untuk 
mengproseskan perasaanmu dahulu.
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
Response sounds awkward, with literal phrasing of 
"heart broken" as "rusak hatinya". The incorrect 
application of affixes ("mengproseskan") also 
sounds unnatural.
d_ID Request: apa sih bedanya antibiotik ama obat biasa? 
Assistant Response: Anti-biotik adalah obat khusus untuk infeksi 
bakteri, sedangkan obat biasa lebih bermacam macam fungsinya.
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
"Anti-" is considered a bound morpheme prefix, and 
according to our LS, there should not be a hyphen 
applied. As for reduplications in our locale, hyphen 
should be applied appropriately, except for Proper 
Names. In this case, it should be "bermacam-
macam".

it_IT Response: 
"Ecco un esempio di curiosità in politica: 
"Qual è la durata più lunga di un presidente degli Stati Uniti?"
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
Although one can intuitively understand the 
meaning of the sentence, the expression "La durata 
più lunga" does not really make sense in this 
context, or it could even lead to a double entendre. 
Suggestion (optional) --> It would be more correct 
to say "Quale presidente ha occupato la carica più a 
lungo nella storia degli Stati Uniti?" or "Qual è stata 
la presidenza più duratura nella storia degli Stati 
Uniti d'America?"
it_IT Response: 
Come modello di linguaggio AI, non ho accesso ai link ipertestuali o ai 
documenti che stanno fuori, come il foglio di calcolo Google che hai 
menzionato. Tuttavia, se puoi fornirmi i numeri delle colonne 1 e 3, posso 
calcolare le loro medie e determinare se la media dei numeri nella terza 
colonna è maggiore della media dei numeri nella prima colonna.
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
"che stanno fuori" is not a correct expression in this 
case, as it is too literal. 
In Italian the correct acronym for "Artificial 
Intelligence" would be IA ("Intelligenza Artificiale") 
and swapping the words sounds awkward. However 
the English acronym is largely used in this type of 
context, so there is no need to flag this as a 
localisation issue.

it_IT Response: 
Ecco un elenco di tutti gli ingredienti della ricetta Fusilli con verdure 
primavera e pangrattato con le loro quantità: 
Copertura della mollica: 
- 1 tazza di pangrattato fresco 
- 1/2 tazza di mandorle a scaglie 
- 1 spicchio d'aglio pressato 
- 1 cucchiaio di burro non salato 
- 1/4 di cucchiaino di sale kosher 
Pasta: 
- 1 chilo di fusilli o altra pasta a spirale 
- 1 mazzetto di asparagi cimati e tagliati a pezzetti di 1 pollice 
- 1/2 tazza di piselli surgelati 
- 2 cucchiai di burro non salato 
- 2 cucchiaini di scorza di limone finemente grattugiata 
- 1 confezione (4 once) di spinaci novelli tritati (circa 4 tazze 
confezionate) 
- 1/2 cucchiaino di sale kosher 
- 1 vaschetta di mascarpone (8 once) (1/2 tazza) 
Copertura della mollica:
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
Punctuation: 
“Una volta sciolto aggiungere le briciole, le mandorle, 
l'aglio e il sale." 
❌
"Una volta sciolto, aggiungere le briciole, le 
mandorle, l'aglio e il sale." 
✅
In this case, the comma after the word "sciolto" is 
necessary to highlight and better explain the 
sequence of actions. 
Awkward or unnatural writing:
"Copertura della mollica". This sentence does not 
convey the correct meaning, as it is the 
breadcrumbs which create the crispy layer, not the 
layer covering the breadcrumbs. 
Units of measurements are not localized:
1. '1 pollice' should have been '1.5 cm'
2. ' (4 once)'should have been '113 gr'
3. '(8 once)' should have been '227 gr'
4. '1/4 di pollice' should have been '6.35 mm’

Copertura della mollica: 
- Se si usa il pane fresco, rimuovere la crosta e tagliarlo a cubetti, quindi 
metterlo nella ciotola del robot da cucina e frullarlo fino ad avere le 
briciole. Sarà necessaria 1 tazza. 
- Nello stesso robot, tritare le mandorle in pezzi più piccoli di 1/4 di 
pollice (in alternativa, tritarle a mano). 
- In una padella grande, riscaldare il burro a fuoco medio. Una volta 
sciolto aggiungere le briciole, le mandorle, l'aglio e il sale. Amalgamare 
per bene gli ingredienti mentre si scaldano. Cuocere mescolando 
spesso, fino a quando non risulterà dorato e flagrante, per circa 4-6 
minuti. Trasferire il tutto in una ciotola media e mettere da parte. 
Nota: la ricetta non include la quantità di pane necessaria per fare 1 
tazza di briciole.

Formatting 
and Tone
Assistant uses inappropriate terminology for fracture 
types:
"圧⼒⻣折" should be "圧迫⻣折".
"ં" should be "捻転⻣折".
"緑⾊ͷ⻣折" should be "若⽊⻣折".
All of the incorrect terms are most likely literal 
translations of the English terminology. 
It also consists of Formatting and Punctuation error. 
Although the round period ( 。) is used at the end of 
Japanese sentences, regular periods ( . ) are used 
for numbering. 
The half-width colon (:) should not be used in our 
locale.

ͷͨΊͷ

Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
"確͔ʹ" means something like "that's true" which 
does not make sense considering the context of the 
complete sentence. 
This is used at the beginning of many responses 
that begin with "here is a summary" and are most 
likely inaccurate translations of the English term 
"sure".

ms_MY Di Cameron Highlands sejauh ini, 
Hujan dan kabus meliputi api, 
T aman-taman berbunga berkilau, 
Menghiasi segala pemandang. 
Ladang teh luas warna hijau, 
Strawberi manis campak lembut, 
Cuaca sejuk berseri indah, 
Menjadi kenangan takkan berulang. 
Di gerai kecil santai berdua, 
T eh panas selimut hangat kali, 
Strawberi segar berasap sedap, 
Menggoda selera belakang lipat. 
Trek hutan mencabar tekad, 
Menjelajahi alam penuh khidmat, 
Bunga-bunga berwarna cantik, 
Menguatkan rasa damai hati.
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
Unnatural Malay phrasing caused by weak 
collocation and semantic mismatch. Although the 
words are Malay and arranged poetically, they do not 
follow natural pairing patterns used by native 
speakers, even in poetic writing.

ms_MY Response: Dari KLIA, anda boleh kenaikan kereta untuk ke Bukit 
Bintang.
Grade: Issues present. Grammar.
Response: Daripada KLIA, anda boleh naik kereta untuk ke Bukit 
Bintang.
Grade: No issues.
Grammar Rationale 1: 
"kenaikan" is incorrectly used in this setence.  
Rationale 2: 
Though the usage of "daripada" is grammatically 
wrong, it conforms to how native speakers speak. 
This is considered acceptable.
ms_MY Example 1: 
Response: Membawa laptop sewaktu melancong merupakan ide yang 
baik.
Grade: Issues present. Wrong Language.
Example 2:
Reponse: Membawa laptop sewaktu melancong merupakan idea yang 
baik.
Grade: No issues.
Wrong 
Language
Rationale 1:  
"ide" is an Indonesian word that cannot be verified in 
Kamus.  
Rationale 2: 
"Laptop" is an example of an acceptable EN word in 
the locale.
ms_MY Berdasarkan senarai yang diberikan, berikut adalah kedai-kedai di Johor 
Bahru yang menyediakan makanan barat:
1. Pondok Santapan Larkin (terutamanya Maggie Goreng dan Maraconi 
Goreng)
2. SDS Cafe (terutamanya American Breakfast, Bagel, Bagel Nasi Lemak, 
dan Western)
3. Kapten Steamboat and Grill (terutamanya nugget, salad, dan ice 
cream)
No Issues Although several English words are present (e.g., Big 
Breakfast, Burger, Western, nugget, salad, ice cream, 
pastries), these terms are commonly used, widely 
understood, and naturally adopted in Malaysian 
Malay. Code-switching between Malay and English 
is culturally acceptable and does not affect 
comprehension.

ms_MY Prompt: Saya rasa sedih... 
Response: Apakah yang telah berlaku sehingga membuatkan kamu 
berasa sedih?
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
While the sentence is grammatically correct, it is 
unnatural as native speakers do not speak formally 
on a day-to-day basis. An example of a natural 
response would be "Kenapa awak sedih? Apa yang 
boleh saya lakukan untuk gembirakan awak?"
ms_MY Prompt: Berapakah jumlah pelajar yang bakal menduduki SPM tahun ini?
Response: 10,000 pelajar akan meduduki N Levels pada tahun ini.
Unlocalized 
Information
Response talked about "N Levels", a national exam 
taken by Singaporean students. This is unrelated to 
the user's prompt and the target locale.
ms_MY Prompt: Bilakah Maulidur Rasul disambut?  
Response: Maulidur Rasul di Malaysia disambut pada 12 Rabi' al-Awwal.
Overly-
localized 
content
Response gives the impression that Maulidur Rasul 
falls on 12 Rabi' al-Awwal only in Malaysia when in 
actuality, Maulidur Rasul is being celebrated on the 
exact same date all around the world.

Groeien' is an intransitive verb in Dutch. If the 
subject (a person) is growing something, the verb is 
'kweken'.

pt_BR Response:
A àgua é uma substância química cujas moléculas são formadas por 
dois átomos de hidrogenio e um de oxigénio. É abundante no universo,

"hidrogênio";

pt_BR"fundo de poupar" 
Response: 
Reserve uma parte do seu dinheiro para cobrir emergências como reparos de 
carro ou contas médicas, evitando dívidas caras. Priorize uma conta poupança 
para essas despesas inesperadas e defina regras para seu uso. Use o fundo de 
poupar em caso de necessidade, mas lembre-se de reconstruí-lo 
posteriormente.
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
The expression "fundo de poupar" is not used in 
Portuguese. In natural writing or speech for the 
pt_BR locale, the correct word in this context is 
"poupança". 
The expression picked up in the response is an 
awkward translation from English.
pt_BR“o osso mais grande" 
Response: 
O osso mais grande na parte superior do corpo é o esterno, um osso plano e 
em forma de escudo localizado no peito. Ele protege muitos órgãos vitais, 
incluindo o coração e os pulmões. O esterno é composto por várias partes, 
incluindo a mancha esternal, as costelas e o processo xifóide. Ele conecta-se a 
muitos outros ossos e fornece um ponto de ancoragem para vários músculos.
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing & 
Grammar
O osso mais grande" is a literal translation from 
"the biggest bone" in English, which in Portuguese 
is unnatural. The correct translation in this case is 
"o maior osso". We should mark this localization 
issue as both Awkward or Unnatural Writing AND 
Grammar issues since it does not comply with 
grammatical rules in pt_BR.

pt_BR "espero que este email o encontre bem"; "com sua diplomação na mão". 
Response: 
Querido professor, 
Espero que este e-mail o encontre bem. 
Queria lhe agradecer por todo apoio dado durante esses anos de 
faculdade. Depois de estar com a minha diplomação na mão, fico mais 
aliviada e com a certeza de que tudo valeu a pena! 
Mais uma vez, obrigada por tudo! 
Atenciosamente, 
Marcela
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
"Espero que este e-mail o encontre bem" is a direct 
translation from English ("hope this e-mail finds you 
well"). Though there is no grammar issue here, this is 
unnatural in pt_BR locale. A better localised 
sentence could be "Espero que esteja tudo bem". 
Also, although the word "diplomação" exists in 
Portuguese, it is not used to describe a student's 
graduation. The word "graduação" is commonly 
used instead. In addition to that, the expressions 
"diplomação na mão" or even "graduação na mão" 
are not sensical in Portuguese, therefore, an 
awkward or unnatural writing.
The expressions "com o meu diploma na mão" 
convey the idea intended in the response using a 
natural Portuguese writing.
pt_BR Response:
Historicamente, enquanto forma de prática física, o ginástica surgiu na 
Pré-História. Contudo, veio a se tornar um modalidade esportiva apenas 
em 1881, em escolas alemãs tipicamente masculinas. Desse modo, a 
ginástica artística consagrou-se como a forma mais antiga do desporto 
e em decorrência disto, sua história é constantemente confundida com a 
da ginástica em si, o que não fere sua evolução artística individual 
posterior.
Grammar "o ginástica" and "um modalidade" are incorrect 
gender inflections in pt_BR. Following grammar 
rules, correct wording is "a ginástica" and "uma 
modalidade". This should be flagged as Grammar 
error under Localization Issues.

pt_BR Aqui está uma lista para os estados do Sudeste: 
- Espírito Santo 
- Minas Gerais 
- Rio de Janeiro 
- São Paulo 
Seguindo a ordem acima, veja abaixo uma expressão comum em cada 
um dos estados: 
1. 
"Ir pro rock: Sair para uma balada. 
2. 
"Fragou a ideia?": Captou a ideia. 
3. 
"O churrasco hoje é 0800": O churrasco hoje é de graça. 
4.  
"Vou fazer isso em dois palitos: Algo que é feito rapidamente.
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation 
& Grammar
In general, bulleted or numbered lists are separated 
either by semi colon or full stop. The formatting of 
the first bulleted list is missing punctuation whereas 
the numbered list is not formatted in a consistent 
way with pt_BR conventions (response skips a line 
between number and sentences). It also presents 
some issues with punctuation in quotation of 
sentences where some sentences lack the closing 
quote. 
In addition to formatting and punctuation issues, 
note that sentences are being capitalized after the 
colon in the numbered list. This is incorrect in the 
locale where sentences should begin in lowercase 
after colon. This should also be flagged under 
Grammar issues.

th_TH Request: วันหยุดคุณชอบไปทำอะไร 
Response: ฉันชอบนอนพัก.

and 
Punctuation

th_TH No issues: 
Request: วันนี้อากาศดีไหม ฉันควรทำอะไรดี
Response: อากาศดีมากเลย! หรือคุณอยากจะให้ฉันวางแผนการท่องเที่ยวให้
ไหม?
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
In the Thai language, the exclamation mark “!” is 
used in expressive situations and “?” (question 
mark) is used at the end of an interrogative 
sentence, or as a substitute for a question word.

uk_UA Prompt: Поясни, що таĸе лінійне програмування.
Response: У задачі лінійного програмування є три основні сĸладові.
Змінні (decision variables):
Це ті значення, яĸі потрібно знайти. Наприĸлад, сĸільĸи товарів 
потрібно виготовити, яĸ розподілити ресурси і т. д.
Цільова фунĸція (objective function): 
Це математичне правило у вигляді лінійного рівняння, яĸе ви хочете 
маĸсимізувати або мінімізувати.
Correct response: У задачі лінійного програмування є три основні 
сĸладові.
Змінні: 
Це ті значення, яĸі потрібно знайти. Наприĸлад, сĸільĸи товарів 
потрібно виготовити, яĸ розподілити ресурси і т. д.
Цільова фунĸція: 
Це математичне правило у вигляді лінійного рівняння, яĸе ви хочете 
маĸсимізувати або мінімізувати.
Other "Змінні", "цільова фунĸція" are common 
mathematical terms in Ukrainian. Adding English 
term in brackets in unnecessary. Since the model is 
not using wrong language as such and more so 
using unnecessary additional description in English, 
this should be marked as Localization > Other - 
Unnecessary addition of English words in a 
response.
vi_VN Prompt: Hãy viết một đoạn văn ngắn nói về lòng yêu nước.

của một tình thần kiên cường không bao giờ lùi bước trước khó khăn…
Spelling The response contains typos “tình thần” .
vi_VN Prompt: Hãy tóm tắt lại bài viết sau “ …có thể nhìn nhận từ bên ngoài về 
sự bản sắc văn hóa dân tộc là ở thuộc tính tinh thần của nó….”

Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
Although the prompt contains the term “sự bản sắc” , 
the response repeats it without correction. The 
response will be penalized for using wording that 
lacks the fluency expected of a native speaker, such 
as an overly literal translation or obvious machine 
translation.

vi_VN Prompt: Hãy viết một đoạn văn ngắn giới thiệu về bún bò Huế.

phú, phản ánh sự giao thoa giữa các tầng lớp xã hội….
Grammar The response contains a paragraph that follows 
English-style structure with three parts connected 
by commas: a main clause, a prepositional phrase 
starting with “với - with” , and a verb-led phrase 
positioned after the comma.
vi_VN Prompt: Hãy cho tôi công thức làm món giò chay.

Unlocalized 
Information
The assistant should recognize that “mì căn” is the 
more relevant and appropriate term than “gluten” in 
our locale.

zh_* No issue: 
1. Opening section (salutation): 
親愛的陳⼩姐： 
2. Dialogue terms (used to indicate a speaker)

Yes, issues present: 
1. Opening section (salutation): 
尊敬的[收件⼈姓名]，
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
In Chinese locales, standard writing conventions 
typically require a colon (：) after the opening 
salutation in formal correspondence. While commas 
are commonly used in English greetings, applying 
the same punctuation in Chinese reflects English 
conventions rather than localized usage.

and 
Punctuation

zh_* 第⼀条⽅程： 
x - 2y = 4 \quad \text（⽅程①）
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
\quad \text’ is an internal mark and it is considered a 
text-level formatting issue. Hence, it should be 
penalized in localization - formatting or punctuation 
issues.
zh_* No issues:  
Uses enumeration commas consistently within the same sentence. 
1. 苹果、橙⼦、⾹蕉、电视、收⾳机 
2. 苹果，橙⼦，⾹蕉，电视，收⾳机 
Yes, issues present:  
Mixes commas and enumeration commas within the same sentence, 
resulting in inconsistent punctuation. 
苹果，橙⼦、⾹蕉、电视，收⾳机
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
While enumeration commas (、) and commas (，) 
serve different functions in standard Chinese writing, 
their usage is commonly mixed or misused in 
modern writing. Because of this, treating every 
occurrence as a localization issue may be overly 
strict. 
A penalty should only be applied when both 
punctuation marks are used inconsistently within the 
same sentence, as this impacts readability and 
consistency. Otherwise, isolated use of either 
punctuation mark should not be classified as a 
localization issue.

zh_* Request: 
什么是⼈⼯智能？ 
Response:
"⼈⼯智能是计算机科学的⼀个分⽀，研究如何让机器执⾏通常需要⼈类
智能的任务。"
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
Full-width punctuation is a standard feature of 
Chinese writing systems and should be applied 
consistently across all zh locales. This includes 
punctuation such as quotation marks, which are 
designed to occupy full-width in line with Chinese 
characters.
The difference between half-width and full-width 
punctuation is also straightforward to verify by copy 
pasting into a text box for comparison. (see image 
below) 
The assistant incorrectly used half-width quotation 
marks (" ")

zh_CN 如果您发现有⼈停放在您的街区的⻋辆 
Response: 
抱歉，我⽆法提供任何关于⾮法⾏为的帮助或建议。如果您发现有⼈停放在您
的街区的⻋辆，您应该先尝试联系当地的交通管理部⻔或警察局，以了解该⻋

Unnatural 
Writing

confusing, which makes the sentence “如果您发

hard to be understood.

zh_CN Example 1: 
我们⼼爱的珍·哈洛在去世后⼏天内离开了我们。 
Example 2:  
除了她在银幕上的成就外，珍还是⼀个亲切、善良的灵魂。 
Response for Example 1 and 2:
好莱坞，加利福尼亚州⸺1937年6⽉9⽇，我们深感悲痛地宣布，我们⼼爱
的珍·哈洛在去世后⼏天内离开了我们。 她26岁。 珍是我们家庭、朋友和⽆
数粉丝的灯塔，她的光芒将永远照亮我们的⽣活。
珍·哈洛于1937年5⽉下旬突发重病，尽管她的意志坚强，但她的身体⽆法承
受。 她的去世是⼀个巨⼤的意外，让我们所有⼈都措⼿不及。
珍·哈洛在好莱坞留下了不可磨灭的印记，她的才华和美貌令所有⼈着迷。 
她是天⽣的明星，她的屏幕魅⼒是⽆与伦⽐的。 从她在《双响屁》中的突破
性⻆⾊到她在《地狱天使》中的标志性表演，珍的天赋是不可否认的。

⼼给每个⼈都带来了快乐。 她对她所爱的⼈的奉献是⽆限的，她的存在将永
远被我们铭记。
Awkward or 
Unnatural 
Writing
Rationale 1: 
It's an awkward expression. “在去世后⼏天内
离开了我们” suggests someone left after 
their death, which is confusing. The overall 
wording and expression are unnatural which 
does not sound like native speakers in the 
current locale. 
Rationale 2: 
It's an unnatural expression in Chinese. In 
our locale, we don't describe someone 
"is"/"是" a soul.

zh_CN 请介绍⼀下债务周期的类型有哪些？ 
Response: 
债务周期是指公司通过借贷和偿还债务来管理其财务义务的时间。 根据
其偿还期限，债务周期可以分为⻓期债务周期和短期债务周期。 
1.⻓期债务周期： 
⻓期债务周期涉及公司为超过⼀年期限的财务承诺筹集资⾦。 这些债务
通常以固定利率提供，并要求定期还款，通常为每年或每五年。 常⻅的
⻓期债务⼯具包括： 
1.公司债券：公司债券是公司向投资者发⾏的债务证券。 这些债券承诺
在未来特定⽇期⽀付固定利率的定期利息，以及到期时归还原始⾯额。 
2.⻓期贷款：公司可以从银⾏或其他⾦融机构获得⻓期贷款，以满⾜其
⻓期资⾦需求。 这些贷款可以有固定或浮动利率，并要求在⼀定期限内
进⾏还款，通常为5年到25年。 
Formatting 
and 
Punctuation
Some sentences end with a Western-style full stop, 
but a Chinese sentence should end with a Chinese 
full stop "。".

•Truthfulness

2.2. Preference Ranking 
•Satisfaction 
Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Examples:
Locale Example Localization 
Issues Explanation
2.短期债务周期： 
短期债务周期涉及公司为⼀年或更短的期限筹集资⾦。 这些债务通常以
浮动利率提供，并要求在短期内还款，通常为30天⾄12个⽉。 常⻅的短
期债务⼯具包括： 
1.商业票据：商业票据是公司向供应商或其他公司发⾏的短期债务证
券。 它们承诺在到期时以折扣价归还原始⾯额。 商业票据通常⽤于管理
公司的流动资⾦需求. 
2.短期贷款：公司可以从银⾏或其他⾦融机构获得短期贷款，以满⾜其
短期资⾦需求。 这些贷款通常有较短的到期期限，从⼏天到⼀年不等，
并可能具有浮动利率，以适应市场条件的变化. 
3.存款：公司可以将资⾦存⼊银⾏，以获得短期利息收⼊。 虽然这些存
款不是债务，但它们可以作为公司管理短期流动资⾦的⼯具. 
总之，⻓期债务周期涉及为超过⼀年期限的财务承诺筹集资⾦，⽽短期
债务周期涉及为⼀年或更短的期限筹集资⾦。 每个债务周期都有其独特
的⻛险和回报特征，公司根据其财务⽬标和可⽤性来决定使⽤哪种债务
⼯具.

2。 短期贷款：公司可以从银⾏或其他⾦融机构获得短期贷款，以满⾜
其短期资⾦需求。 这些贷款通常有较短的到期期限，从⼏天到⼀年不
等，并可能具有浮动利率，以适应市场条件的变化.
3。 存款：公司可以将资⾦存⼊银⾏，以获得短期利息收⼊。 虽然这些
存款不是债务，但它们可以作为公司管理短期流动资⾦的⼯具.
总之，⻓期债务周期涉及为超过⼀年期限的财务承诺筹集资⾦，⽽短期
债务周期涉及为⼀年或更短的期限筹集资⾦。 每个债务周期都有其独特
的⻛险和回报特征，公司根据其财务⽬标和可⽤性来决定使⽤哪种债务
⼯具.

appropriate and constitutes unnecessary language mixing.

English vocabulary within a Chinese passage is culturally 
acceptable and should not be penalized.

colloquial or mixed style, the response should follow 
the locale’s culture. In formal or presentation

expected. Therefore, using colloquial language is 
inappropriate and should be penalized.

申請⽂件（application documents）

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Concision (Verbosity) 
Responses should address the users' needs in a well-written and efficient way. This dimension focuses on the following: distractions and 
length restrictions specified by the user.: 
1 - Distractions 
A “good” response should be free of distractions. Distractions refer to any content in the response that deviates from the main topic or 
request, making it harder to find the information the user needs. Distraction negatively affects the readability of the response adding 
unnecessary information and making it longer or eliminating the necessary information making the response too short. 
Distractions can take many forms: 
•Anecdotes or side stories that are unrelated to the main topic. 
•Excessive use of technical jargon or complex language may confuse the reader. 
•Unnecessary and excessive background information that doesn’t directly address the user’s request. 
•Excessive use of filler words 
•Repetition of words or phrases in the context of the request 
Step 2. Response Evaluation 
2.1 Single Response Rating - Concision (verbosity)
--- Page 165 ---
NAVIGATION BAR

STEP 2. RESPONSE 
EV ALUATION 
2.1 Single Response Rating: 
•Following Instructions
•Localization
•Concision
•Truthfulness 
•Satisfaction
2.2. Preference Ranking 
•Satisfaction 
Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
2 - Length restrictions specified by the user in the prompt 
•A response should be proportionate to the prompt’s expectations (not too excessive and not too short) 
•T oo excessive: hard to understand and stay focused while reading 
•To o  s h o r t: undermines the meaning and reading quality, leaving the reader expecting more 
•A response should address the users’ needs by providing relevant details/explanations to address the user’s instructions 
•A well-written response provides a balance of need-to-know information, followed by nice-to-know information when appropriate. 
•Need-to-know: the direct answer to the user’s question 
•Nice-to-know: supporting details, context, evidence, rationale, qualifiers. 
•Following the length restrictions specified in the user prompt is critical to make a correct grading. 
•Case 1: The user explicitly states instructions that affect concision, such as: “One stanza” , “3 lines” , “200 words” , “in detail” , “in short” , “five 
paragraphs long” , etc. 
•
⚠ NOTE: There are cases when concision evaluation criteria are not fully applicable. For example, a 500-word response might be interpreted as 
not concise. However, if the user explicitly requested it, we rate it as concise (Good) if it also fits the "distractions" requirements.  
•Case 2: The user does not mention any explicit requirements for the response length. In such cases, a long (or short) response can still be “good” 
as long as the response fits the characteristics above. For example:  
•“T ell me more about xxx” 
•“Explain xxx to me”

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scale for Single Response Rating
Good The concision is "Good" if: 
•The response is free from distractions and focuses on the requested information, does not detract from the 
main topic. 
•Length restrictions are followed.
Bad The concision is "Bad" if: 
•The response contains multiple distractions that make it difficult for the user to find the relevant information. 
•The response is excessively long and verbose based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request 
(if applicable). 
•The response is too short based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if applicable).
Acceptable The concision is "Acceptable" if:  
•The response contains minor distractions that do not make it difficult for the user to find the relevant 
information. 
•The response is slightly longer based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if applicable). 
•The response is slightly shorter based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if applicable).
Concision should not be mistaken for Following Instructions. Although these two dimensions are closely related, there 
is no exact match in rating concision and instruction following. The Concision is an independent evaluation criterion.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Upon selecting "Bad" or "Acceptable" options, the follow-up question will show up: 
•It could have been made shorter: the response could have been edited for clarity/remove repetition and as a consequence made 
shorter 
•It could have been made longer: the response is too terse and could have been made longer to make it more readable or better 
sounding or have more relevant and helpful information. 
⚠ NOTE: Do not penalize the response based on the length of another response. A longer response can still be rated as "Good" (Concise) 
if follows the instructions about distractions and length restrictions. Consider each response individually.

Examples of Single Response Rating
User Request Response Rating Rationale
Can you explain the 
process of 
photosynthesis?
Photosynthesis is the transformative process in which green plants 
absorb light energy and convert it into chemical energy (glucose). 
Chlorophyll plays an important role in trapping light energy to support 
the conversion of carbon dioxide and water into glucose which is the 
primary form of food for green plants. This process occurs within plant 
cells containing chloroplasts, where chlorophyll is stored. Water is 
absorbed through the roots, while carbon dioxide enters through the 
stomata. The culmination of these reactions produce glucose. 
Photosynthesis is crucial for the survival of plants, as it provides them 
with the energy they need to grow and produce oxygen, which is 
essential for many living organisms, including humans.
Acceptable - It could 
have been made shorter
There are minor 
distractions, but they 
don't significantly 
detract from the main 
content - while 
paragraph one explains 
the process with all the 
necessary information 
the second pargraph 
includes extra information 
that the user request did 
not ask for.

Photosynthesis is the transformative process in which green plants absorb light 
energy and convert it into chemical energy (glucose). Chlorophyll plays an 
important role in trapping light energy to support the conversion of carbon 
dioxide and water into glucose which is the primary form of food for green 
plants. This process occurs within plant cells containing chloroplasts, where

enters through the stomata. The culmination of these reactions produce

Photosynthesis is crucial for the survival of plants, as it provides them with the

have been

excessively long and

I am looking for 
examples of renewable 
energy
Renewable energy is energy derived from natural resources that are 
replenished on a human timescale. Here are some examples of renewable 
energy sources: 
1. Solar Energy - An energy source that harnesses the power of the sun by 
converting sunlight into electricity using semiconductors. 
2. Wind Energy - An energy source that harnesses the power of wind to 
generate electricity using wind turbines. 
3. Hydroelectric Energy - An energy source that uses the force of moving 
water to generate electricity through dams and turbines 
4. Geothermal Energy - An energy source that harnesses the Earth's 
natural heat to produce electricity and provide heating and cooling 
5. Biomass Energy - Organic matter like wood, crops, or manure can be 
burned to produce heat and electricity or converted into biofuels for 
transportation. 
6. Hydrogen Energy - Renewable sources like biomass and solar power 
can be used to produce hydrogen gas, which can then be stored and 
used as a clean fuel. 
7. Tidal and Wave Energy - An energy source that converts the kinetic 
energy of waves and tides into electricity
Acceptable -  It could 
have been made 
shorter
There are minor 
distractions, but they 
don't significantly 
detract from the main 
content.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Truthfulness  
Every answer must be truthful. The truthful response must be factually and contextually correct. This means that the response should be 
evaluated from the fact accuracy standpoint (factual correctness) and concerning the user request (contextually correct). Let's take a 
closer look at both aspects of evaluation.  
1. Factual Correctness 
A factually correct response contains verifiable information such as general facts, historical facts, math and reasoning, etc. In other words, 
this is the information that is known in the world (e.g., people, events, geographical subjects, historical events, and so on). 
T o verify the accuracy of the information in the response, conduct a web search.
May or May Not be Truthful
Asses the response truthfulness (accuracy) for the following requests:
May or May Not be 
Truthful User Request Response Truthfulness
General Facts Have wild orcas killed 
people? tell me an 
example
The most infamous example of an orca attack on 
humans is the case of Dave Pearson, a surfer who 
was killed by a pod of orcas off the coast of New 
South Wales, Australia in 1972. The incident is 
believed to be the first documented case of orcas 
killing a human in the wild.
The text in red is false and there has been no 
incidents of wild orcas killing people. This is 
an example of an inaccurate answer (this can 
also be called hallucination - because this 
example never existed in the real world).

Non-Fiction Creative 
Writing
•Fact-based and Realistic: Anchored in reality, non-
fiction covers true events, people, and facts, relying on 
research and historical accuracy. 
•Purpose: It seeks to educate and inform, offering 
insights into various domains like history, science, and 
biographies, facilitating a deeper understanding of the 
world. 
•Sticking to the Facts: Non-fiction prioritizes accuracy 
and objectivity, though it can also encompass subjective 
narratives like memoirs or opinion pieces.
Creative works (poems, stories, essays, etc.) may be 
fiction or non-fiction. The non-fiction type of creative 
works always needs to be assessed for accuracy.

In this example, putting out electric fires with 
water is hazardous and a piece of creative 
writing that does say how to do the above, is 
taking liberal (and harmful) license with reality. 
This should be marked as Not Truthful.
Additional Considerations 
•Time Sensitive Requests. When assessing a response to a time-sensitive request, consider the request is being made in the present 
moment. Rate the response based on its accuracy and relevance to today's circumstances. 
•Context. When context (previous conversation between User and Assistant) is present, accuracy should be rated taking context into 
account. For example, you should consider any conditions/requirements/restrictions explicitly mentioned or implied in the previous 
conversation.

STEP 3. COMMENTS

Always Truthful 
When the concept of accuracy is not applicable, the response should be marked as "Truthful". The following categories fall under this 
rating:  
•Fictional creative writing. If the user requests to write a fictional essay with a given plot, factual accuracy is not applicable here 
(unless specifically asked by the user or it is implicit). Examples of fictional (creative works) include poems, stories, essays, reports, 
recipes, or anything that requires creative license. For example:  
•“Write a poem about a falling star in 5 lines”  
•Imagination and Invention: Fiction is defined by its creative storytelling, involving made-up characters, settings, and plots, 
diverging from factual events. 
•Purpose: Mainly to entertain, fiction transports readers into varied realms, exploring emotions and ideas through imaginative 
narratives across genres like romance, sci-fi, and more. 
•Flexibility with Fact: It permits authors to alter reality, including time and space, enhancing the story's appeal and depth 
•Accuracy does not apply to responses declining to address the user's request 
•Data Limitations. Sometimes a model is unable to address the user request due to data limitations.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
NOT Truthful 
A factually INCORRECT response is the one that contains the non-verifiable information:  
•Opinion-seeking requests cannot be verified, hence, such responses are not truthful.  
2. Contextual Correctness  
When the user provides some reference text, the assistant's response must be based on the information mentioned in the given text:   
•Summarization or re-writing request 
•The assistant’s response must be truthful to the contextual text, a response with information not mentioned in the original text is not truthful 
(even if it is factually accurate). 
•Q&A from a provided body of text 
•“[story between dog and boy] Given the story above, answer the following questions…” . 
•A response containing wrong answers is not truthful. 
•If the response is based on some information not mentioned in the given text, it is not truthful either. 
•Reference text may come from previous conversations between the user and the Assistant, either in previous user request or in previous 
responses from the assistant. 
⚠ Primary information vs. secondary information:  
Primary information is more important as it is essential to address the user’s request, while secondary information refers to details and explanations that 
support the primary information.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scale for Single Response Rating 
Truthful •The response is factually correct, and accurate given the contextual text (if provided).  
•For math/reasoning requests, both the final answer and the reasoning are correct.  
•For coding requests, the code should be correct in logic and outputs, including in edge cases.
Partially Truthful •The primary information is factually correct, and accurate given the contextual text (if provided). But the 
secondary information is inaccurate. 
•For math/reasoning requests, the final answer is correct but the reasoning is incorrect.  
•For coding requests, the code might fail in edge cases but code's logic is correct.
Not Truthful •The primary information is factually incorrect, or inaccurate given the contextual text (if provided). 
•For math/reasoning requests, reasoning steps are wrong. 
•For coding requests, the code has mistakes that will lead to incorrect outputs.

User request Response Truthfulness Notes
User: Can you tell me some fun facts about cats? 
Assistant: Sure, here are some fun facts about cats: 
- Cats spend nearly one-third of their lives cleaning themselves: Cats have 
developed this behavior to distribute natural oils throughout their fur, maintain 
their coat's health, and remove parasites.- 
- Cats can’t taste sweetness: Cats lack the sweet taste receptors on their 
tongues, which means they may not be as interested in sweet treats as dogs 
and humans.- 
- A cat’s sense of smell is 14 times stronger than humans: This heightened 
sense of smell helps them hunt, detect prey, and recognize their owners. 
- Every cat has a unique whisker pattern: Just like human fingerprints, a cat's 
whisker pattern is distinctive and can be used to identify them. 
User: That's great but too long. Make it more concise by using 3 sentences. No 
bullet points please.
Cats are fascinating 
creatures with many unique 
traits. From their ability to 
clean themselves constantly 
and jump up to six times 
their height, to their 
remarkable sense of smell 
and unique whisker patterns, 
cats demonstrate their 
adaptability and charm. Cats 
have also held significant 
roles in human history, being 
revered and worshipped in 
ancient Egypt and beloved 
companions today.
Partially Truthful The last sentence in the 
summary is not mentioned 
in the previous assistant 
response. Hence, it is a 
hallucination, making the 
response partially truthful.
⚠  The information provided in the response should be relevant to the user in a specific locale. For example:  
•give me a poem on rules for filing taxes - the response can be different for a UK user vs. a US user 
•how much does it cost to run a water heater - the response can differ in terms of the cost itself and the unit of measurement 
•what's the highest position in the government - depending on the locale the truthful response will be different.

Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Satisfaction 
Satisfaction is a holistic rating based on all 4 dimensions covered in previous sections, including Harmfulness: 
•Following Instructions 
•Localization 
•Concision (verbosity) 
•Truthfulness 
Note: If any of the above categories are rated below the highest rank, this would downgrade the Response Satisfaction.  
Satisfaction also includes other important features: 
1.Relevance: The response meets the user's needs (e.g., if requested to produce a song in 5 lines about a furry dog, the response is a 
creative, entertaining song of 5 lines). For coding problems, the code should execute and have good performance characteristics.  
2.Comprehensiveness: The response addresses the entire user request, does not miss important aspects, and includes nice-to-have 
details and explanations. 
3.Formatting: The response is formatted in a pleasing-to-read way (e.g., with the use of bold text, bullet points, and numbered lists). 
4.The code should be readable (with good variable/function names) and formatted correctly. 
5.Creativity: not all responses demand creativity, but if they do (e.g., text generation of poems and short stories) the answers should 
demonstrate novel story plots, and written form/structure. The text should have a flow and not be mechanical.

2.1 Single Response Rating - Satisfaction

•Localization
•Concision
•Truthfulness 
•Satisfaction
2.2. Preference Ranking 
•Satisfaction 
Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
6.Style, Tone, & Grammar: The response should have a helpful tone, not chatty, not be too technical, not have so much jargon such that it 
is difficult to read, and be free of opinion. Responses should possess an engaging writing style that draws the attention of the reader 
without them giving up on the answer. The text is well-written with correct grammar including punctuation and no typos. 
7.Helpful Disengagement: Politely turns down the user request and explains why, if the task is beyond the knowledge or capacity of the 
digital assistant (or literally answering the request would lead to an offensive or dangerous response). 
8.Seek Clarification: Asks a helpful, clarifying question to better understand exactly what the user is asking for if the user request or 
intent is ambiguous, or the user request is lacking context. This also implies that the assistant does not make important assumptions 
that were not already specified in the user request. 
9.Contextual Fit: If the context is present, the response should fit into the context and take the context into account. 
Even though we have not rated these features, consider them while providing the Satisfaction rating.

Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Highly Satisfying The response satisfies all the user’s needs, including: 
•Follows ALL the instructions. 
•Answers ALL the questions. 
•Content is completely truthful. 
•Response is appropriate and does not engage with an offensive/dangerous prompt.  
•Structure is concise and in a pleasing-to-read format. 
•Language is clear, easily understandable, and logically coherent (not repetitive or too technical). 
•T ext is well-written with correct grammar including punctuation and no typos. 
•Includes ALL the important information of the original text segment when the user asks for a summary or rewrite of 
a text segment. 
•Content is creative in brainstorming or creative writing requests. 
•(If context is provided) Response appropriately accounts for the context. 
For Coding domain: 
•The code is self-contained, executable, and readable (good formatting, good variable and function naming, well-
structured, following industry standards). 
•The code has the correct output as requested by the user. 
•Any non-trivial code should be well-documented. 
•The code is optimized in terms of time and space complexity. 
•The code handles edge cases well.
Scale for Single Response Rating

Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Slightly Satisfying The response is overall helpful and appropriate (not offensive/dangerous) to the user, except for some minor issues such 
as: 
•Fails to follow some minor parts of the instructions in the user request. 
•Misses some minor information or details that is necessary to address the user request. 
•Contains some unnecessary information, but users can still easily find the information they requested. 
•Some minor grammar or formatting issues. 
•Note: "If the response contains any grammar or punctuation errors that are not considered localization issues, 
this should be rated as Slightly Unsatisfying or Slightly Satisfying, depending on the severity. (This applies only 
to the en_US locale)   
•Mostly truthful except for some minor details that are not essential to address the user request. 
•Asks a helpful, clarifying question to better understand exactly what the user is asking for if the user request or 
intent is ambiguous, or the user request is lacking context.Slightly UnsatisfyingThe response is only partly helpful to the user but is still appropriate (not offensive/dangerous) with major issues such 
as: 
•Fails to follow some major parts of the instructions in the user request. 
•Misses some important information that is necessary to address the user request. 
•Contains much unnecessary information, making it difficult for a reader to find the information requested. 
•Does not fully understand the intention of the user request and responds with an answer that might be correct, 
follows instructions but was not relevant to the user. 
•Composed or formatted in a way that makes it unpleasing to read. 
•Based on a different but relevant interpretation of the user request. 
•Untruthful in some information that is essential to address the user request. 
•Math/reasoning has correct answers but misleading/incorrect explanations.

Highly Unsatisfying The response is completely unhelpful due to any of the following issues. 
•Response engages with an offensive/dangerous prompt. 
•Contains gibberish. 
•Math/reasoning has wrong answers. 
•Written in languages that are NOT requested by the user. 
•Note: If Wrong Language is selected as a Localization issue, the Satisfaction rating should be determined based 
on severity. If the majority of the response is in the wrong language, select Highly Unsatisfying. If there is 
minimal language mixing, use your judgment to choose the appropriate rating.Answers a completely different 
question from the user’s request. 
•Completely fails to (or fails on nearly all) follow the instructions in the user request. 
•The response is completely not Truthful. For example: 
◦Provides inaccurate information that is essential to address the user request. 
◦Makes up details that were not part of the original text segment, in cases where the user request asks the digital 
assistant to summarize or rewrite a text segment.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scenario Single Response Rating
Ambiguous request or missing contextIdeal response is to ask for clarification or more information, which should be rated as Slightly Satisfying. 
Incomplete response Response can NEVER be rated as Highly Satisfying. Depending on the quality of the response, it can be at 
most Slightly Satisfying.
Offensive or Dangerous request •Flagging: If you encounter content or interactions that you believe could be considered inappropriate, 
culturally irrelevant, or offensive/dangerous, please flag it in the comments section. Be specific about the 
nature of the content and why you believe it needs attention. 
•Penalizing Engagement: For prompts that request Offensive or Dangerous information, the correct 
action is refusal. Any attempt to provide information fully or partially should be penalised for Satisfaction. 
Consider this a significant detractor from a satisfying user experience. 
•Rewarding Disengagement: Response declining to fulfill the request due to safety reasons can still be 
Slightly Satisfying or even Highly Satisfying
Response not following some 
instructions in user request
Response can only be AT MOST Slightly Satisfying. 
• If the response fails to follow instructions completely, it should be rated as Highly Unsatisfying. 
• If the response follows some instructions but not all, it can be rated as Slightly Unsatisfying 
depending on its content quality.
Long vs. concise responses You should penalize responses that are too verbose, containing unnecessary/redundant information.
Special Considerations

Scenario Single Response Rating
Formatting issues N/A
Non-engaging response 
(Unable to address the user request, 
e.g., by saying the request is beyond its 
capacity or knowledge, or asking the 
user for additional information.)
Non-engaging response should be rated as at most Slightly Satisfying (unless the prompt-response is 
related to "Offensive or Dangerous request" ) 
• If user request is ambiguous and missing context, a response asking for clarification or more 
information can be rated as Slightly Satisfying. 
• Otherwise, non-engaging response should be Highly Unsatisfying, as it does not satisfy the user’s 
need. 
Exceptions to this are non engaging responses when the topic is related to Offensive or Dangerous 
content. See "Offensive or Dangerous request"  section above.
Data Limitations 
(Unable to address the user request due 
to data limitation)
User Request: What was the global GDP growth rate in 2023? 
Response A: I'm sorry, I only have data available until 2022. 
Response B: The global GDP growth rate in 2023 was estimated at 3.5%. 
The accurate responses are always more satisfying compared to responses declining the request due to 
data availability. Responses that are made-up or inaccurate at the time of grading should be ranked the 
least satisfying.

Time Sensitive Requests Assume that the request is being made right now. Evaluate and rate the response based on how

response.
Response With Model Identity Do not take into consideration the model identity (OpenAI,etc.) when evaluating the response

RESOURCES 
Summary:  
•If any dimension is the lowest rating (Not Following Instructions, Bad Concision, Not Truthful), then Satisfaction for the overall response 
can only be Slightly Unsatisfying or Highly Unsatisfying.  
•If any dimension, is less than the highest rating (Fully Following Instructions, Good Concision, and Clearly Truthful) and Harmfulness is 
"Not Harmful" then Satisfaction for the overall response cannot be Highly Satisfying. The response can only be Slightly Satisfying or less.

Satisfaction Tooling Logic 
It is important to note that the allowable options for Satisfaction Rating submissions may change depending on your prior selection for the 
various dimensions. If you have selected a Satisfaction Rating option that is not allowed according to the Satisfaction logic defined above, 
you will receive an error message when you attempt to submit the task.
Use the Response Evaluation Summary table that populates based on your dimension selection to help you make your Satisfaction rating.

money

including:

spending on non-essential items 
3. Increasing your income, for example by asking

investment products.

How many planets are in our solar

User Request Response Satisfaction Rating Notes
How to make brownie T o make brownies, you will need the following ingredients: 1 
and 3/4 cups of sugar, 1 and 3/4 cups of all-purpose flour, 3/4 
cup of unsweetened cocoa powder, 1 teaspoon of baking 
powder, 1 teaspoon of salt, 1 cup of melted butter, 4 large 
eggs, 1 teaspoon of vanilla extract, and 1 cup of semi-sweet 
chocolate chips. 
Here is a recipe for making brownies: 
1. Preheat your oven and grease an 8x8 inch baking dish. 
2. In a large mixing bowl, whisk together the sugar, flour, cocoa 
powder, baking powder, and salt. 
3. Add the melted butter, eggs, and vanilla extract to the mixing 
bowl and stir until well combined. 
4. Stir in the chocolate chips. 
5. Pour the batter into the prepared baking dish and smooth 
the top with a spatula. 
6. Bake the brownies for 30-35 minutes, or until a toothpick 
inserted into the center comes out clean. 
7. Let the brownies cool in the pan for at least 30 minutes 
before slicing and serving.
Slightly Satisfying The response addresses 
most of the user request, 
except that it doesn't 
specify the temperature 
of the oven.

User Request Response Satisfaction Rating Notes
Pros and cons of hiking Pros of hiking: 
1. Physical fitness: Hiking can be an excellent workout for your 
body, improving cardiovascular health, building strength, and 
increasing flexibility. 
2. Mental health: Hiking in nature has been shown to reduce 
stress, anxiety, and depression, improving mental well-being. 
3. Socializing: Hiking can be a social activity, allowing you to 
connect with friends, family, or other hikers. 
4. Exploration: Hiking allows you to explore new places and

trade. Policies like the New Deal in the United States, which included public 
works projects, financial reforms, and social programs, helped alleviate the

increase in oil prices due to the OPEC oil embargo. It led to stagflation, a 
combination of high unemployment and inflation. Economic recovery was 
achieved through monetary policies to control inflation, increased 
productivity, and a shift towards energy conservation.

financial crisis worldwide. Recovery efforts included fiscal stimulus, monetary 
easing, and financial reforms. Countries around the world implemented 
various stimulus packages and bailouts to stabilize financial markets and

Sure, I can help you with that! Please let me know which subject you need 
notes on. 
I would like to share some note taking strategies:
* Use headings and subheadings: Divide your notes into sections using 
headings and subheadings. This makes it easier to scan your notes and find 
the information you need.
* Use bullet points: Bullet points are a great way to break down complex 
information into smaller, more manageable chunks. Use them to list out key 
points or to summarize a longer section of notes.
* Use numbers and letters: Use numbers and letters to identify different items 
or ideas within your notes. This can help you keep track of them more easily 
and can also make it easier to organize your notes later.
* Use abbreviations and acronyms: Use abbreviations and acronyms to 
shorten longer words and phrases. This can make your notes easier to read 
and can also save space.
* Use white space: Use white space to make your notes more visually 
appealing and easier to read. This can also help you to organize your notes 
more effectively.
* Use a consistent format: Use a consistent format throughout your notes. 
This can make it easier to review your notes and can also help you to quickly 
recognize important information.

unnecessarily long 
with lots of 
information that is 
not requested by

User Request Response Satisfaction Rating Notes
I have two lists and I would 
like to find the intersection 
of them. Can you write me a 
c++ function to do this?
Here is an example C++ code to find the intersection 
of two lists:
#include <vector> 
 std::vector<int> findIntersection(std::vector<int> 
list1, std::vector<int> list2) { 
    std::vector<int> intersection; 
    for (int element : list1) { 
        for (int element2 : list2) { 
            if (element == element2) { 
                intersection.push_back(element); 
            } 
        } 
    } 
    return intersection; 
Slightly Satisfying The C++ function is correct and returns the 
desired intersection. However, the code can 
be further optimized in terms of time 
complexity by using map or ordered_map.

Here's an example Python code to draw 60 random

import numpy as np 
# Define the simulation parameters 
population_size = 20000 
sample_size = 60 
 population = 1 / np.logspace(0.001, 10, 
population_size) 
indices = np.random.randint(0, population_size, 
size=sample_size) 
samples = population[indices] 
sample_mean = np.mean(samples) 
print(f'Sample mean is {sample_mean:.3f}')

Simplify the expression 
5x(2x + 3) - 4x(x + 1) + 
2x^2 - x^2 + 6x - 3
Sure thing. Here's the solution for you: 
Step 1: Distribute the multiplication  
5x(2x + 3) - 4x(x + 1) + 2x^2 - x^2 + 6x - 3 
= 10x^2 + 15x - 4x^2 - 4x + 2x^2 - x^2 + 6x - 3 
Step 2: Group all the terms containing x^2 and x 
together. 
= (10x^2 - 4x^2 - x^2) + (15x - 4x + 6x) - 3 
Step 3: Simplify each group to get the final answer. 
= 7x^2 + 17x - 3
Slightly UnsatisfyingWhile the final answer is correct, Step 2 
makes a mistake by omitting the 2x^2 term.

User Request Response Satisfaction Rating Notes
Two balls, each with a mass 
of 10 kg, are traveling 
toward each other. Ball A is 
traveling at 20 m/s, and Ball 
B is traveling at 30 m/s. 
They collide and stick 
together. What is the final 
velocity of the two balls 
immediately after the 
collision?
We can solve this using the principles of conservation 
of kinetic energy. It states that the total kinetic energy 
before the collision must equal the total kinetic 
energy after the collision. 
T otal kinetic energy before collision = (1/2) m_a * 
v_a^2 + (1/2) m_b * v_b^2 
= (1/2) 10 kg * (20 m/s)^2 + (1/2) 10 kg * (30 m/s)^2 
=  6500 kg * (m/2)^2 
T otal kinetic energy after collision = (1/2) (m_a + m_v) 
* v_f^2 
= (1/2) (10 kg + 10 kg) * v_f^2 
= 10 kg * v_f^2 
Setting the total kinetic energy before the collision 
equal to the total kinetic energy after the collision, we 
get: 
6500 kg * (m/2)^2 = 10 kg * v_f^2 
Solving for v_f gives the final velocity of the two balls 
immediately after the collision: 
v_f = sqrt( 6500 kg * (m/2)^2 /  10 kg ) = 25.50 m/s
Highly UnsatisfyingThe response is completely incorrect since it 
uses the wrong principles (the principle of 
conservation of momentum should be used 
instead).

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Satisfaction 
Satisfaction Scale for Two Responses 
Preference ranking requires you to compare two responses, and determine which one is more satisfying than the other, based upon all of the 
dimensions you rated in the single response rating. 
Principles for Satisfaction Ranking 
When comparing the responses, please consider all the dimensions (Harmfulness, Truthfulness, Following Instructions, Concision, Satisfaction).  
•If one of the responses is rated highly on all dimensions, it will be ranked as better. 
•If one of the responses possesses fewer of these attributes (or is ranked lower), it will be ranked as worse. 
“Truthful” responses are more important than other attributes 
•Response that are Truthful should be ranked better than the response that is Untruthful. 
•When two responses have similar level of satisfaction, prefer the more truthful response. 
Always use your best judgment.  
•In the end, it can sometimes be very challenging to make decisions about these ranking differentiations.  
Clarifying questions to ask yourself to help to determine the better response:  
•Which response would be less likely to cause harm to an actual user? 
•If YOU were the user who made this user request, which response would YOU rather receive 
•When you make this decision, prioritize Truthfulness.

Tied Ranking: Even after you’ve spent time carefully examining two or more responses, and you’re still completely unsure how to 
differentiate the ranks of two (or more) responses, you can mark these responses as “Same” (i.e., belonging to the same rank).  
Choose the same ranking if:  
•if two responses are duplicates. 
•if you cannot determine that one response is better than another based on the above dimensions.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scale Criteria
Much Better Choose this option if one response addresses the user request while the other one does not. And, it doesn't engage with an 
inappropriate, culturally irrelevant, offensive, or dangerous request.
Better
Choose this option if both responses address the user request, but one is more satisfying in terms of some major aspects, such as: 
• One response is better in following instructions 
• One response provides more truthful information that is essential to address the user’s request 
• One response avoids inappropriate, culturally irrelevant, offensive, or dangerous content. 
• One response is too wordy 
• One response is a lot better to read (including formatting, style)
Slightly Better
Choose this option if both responses address the user request, but one is more satisfying in terms of some minor aspects, such as: 
• One response is slightly better in following instructions. For example, both responses fails to follow some instructions, but one 
response is lightly closer to the user’s requirements. 
• One response provides more truthful information that is non-essential to address the user’s request 
• One response is slightly wordier 
• Some minor spelling, grammar, formatting issues
Same Choose this option if two responses have the same level of satisfaction. For example, two responses are equally helpful or unhelpful.
Satisfaction Scale for Preference Ranking of Two Responses

RESOURCES 
Scale Criteria
Ambiguous request or missing context Prefer the response asking for clarification or more information. 
For example, if the user request is "what is my tax rate?", a non-engaging response is better 
than a response that assumes the user is in T exas or asking about state income tax.
Incomplete response Prefer the response that is more complete.
Offensive or Dangerous request •Flagging: If you encounter content or interactions that you believe could be considered 
inappropriate, culturally irrelevant, or offensive/dangerous, please flag it in the comments 
section. Be specific about the nature of the content and why you believe it needs attention. 
•Prefer the response that doesn't engage with an inappropriate, culturally irrelevant, or 
offensive/dangerous request.
Response not following some instructions in user 
request
Prefer the response that adheres more to the user instructions. 
For example, when user requests “Rewrite the given text using three sentences. T ext: 
<some long text>” , a rewriting with three sentences should be ranked better than the one 
with four sentences, followed by the response not rewriting any text at all.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scale Criteria
Long vs. concise responses Do NOT rank responses solely based on their length. A longer response is NOT necessarily 
more satisfying. Prefer the response that is more concise and to-the-point, while still 
satisfying the user’s need.
Formatting issues Do NOT rank responses solely based on their formatting. A better formatted response is 
NOT necessarily more satisfying or truthful. Please prefer the response that provides more 
helpful information even if it has minor formatting flaws. 
When other aspects are comparable, please prefer the one that is more satisfying due to its 
better formatting.
Non-engaging response 
(Unable to address the user request, e.g., by 
saying the request is beyond its capacity or 
knowledge, or asking the user for additional 
information.)
•If user request is ambiguous and missing context, prefer the response asking for 
clarification or more information. 
•Otherwise, prefer the response providing more helpful information.
Both responses are Highly Unsatisfying •The "less unsatisfying" response can be rated as Slightly Better. 
•If both are equally unsatisfying, choose Same.
Time Sensitive Requests Assume the request is made right now. When ranking, prioritize the response that is the 
most accurate as of today.

Summarize the following text 
using one sentence: 
"The Great Depression was an 
economic downturn that had a 
profound impact on the United 
States and the rest of the world. 
Beginning in 1929, the 
depression lasted until the late 
1930s, causing widespread 
unemployment, poverty, and 
economic despair. The stock 
market crash of 1929, 
combined with a decline in 
international trade and a failure 
of the banking system, played a 
significant role in triggering the 
depression. Despite efforts by 
the government to alleviate the 
suffering, it was not until the 
outbreak of World War II that 
the U.S. economy began to 
recover.”
The Great Depression 
was an economic 
downturn that caused 
severe unemployment, 
poverty, and economic 
despair in the United 
States and lasted from 
1929 to the late 
1930s. The stock 
market crash of 1929, 
the decline in 
international trade, 
and the failure of the 
banking system were 
significant factors in 
triggering the 
depression.
The Great Depression was a severe and prolonged 
economic downturn that affected most countries 
worldwide, beginning in the United States in 1929 and 
lasting until the late 1930s. In the United States, it started 
with the stock market crash on October 29, 1929, known 
as Black Tuesday, when the Dow Jones Industrial Average 
fell by nearly 25%. This event led to widespread panic and 
a sudden decline in consumer confidence, causing many 
investors to lose their life savings. 
The Great Depression had far-reaching consequences 
and impacted various aspects of life. The collapse of the 
stock market led to a decline in business investment, 
causing many companies to go bankrupt and resulting in 
high unemployment rates. As people lost their jobs, they 
also lost their homes and savings, leading to widespread 
poverty and homelessness. The unemployment rate

I would like to share some note taking strategies:
* Use headings and subheadings: Divide your notes into sections 
using headings and subheadings. This makes it easier to scan your 
notes and find the information you need.
* Use bullet points: Bullet points are a great way to break down 
complex information into smaller, more manageable chunks. Use them 
to list out key points or to summarize a longer section of notes.
* Use numbers and letters: Use numbers and letters to identify 
different items or ideas within your notes. This can help you keep track 
of them more easily and can also make it easier to organize your notes 
later.
* Use abbreviations and acronyms: Use abbreviations and acronyms 
to shorten longer words and phrases. This can make your notes easier 
to read and can also save space. 
* Use white space: Use white space to make your notes more visually 
appealing and easier to read. This can also help you to organize your 
notes more effectively.
* Use a consistent format: Use a consistent format throughout your 
notes. This can make it easier to review your notes and can also help 
you to quickly recognize important information.

I am looking

Renewable energy is energy derived from natural resources that are replenished on a human timescale. Here are some 
examples of renewable energy sources: 
Solar Energy - An energy source that harnesses the power of the sun by converting sunlight into electricity using 
semiconductors. 
Wind Energy - An energy source that harnesses the power of wind to generate electricity using wind turbines. 
Hydroelectric Energy - An energy source that uses the force of moving water to generate electricity through dams and 
turbines 
Nuclear Energy - Nuclear power plants use nuclear fission reactions to heat water and produce steam, which drives turbines 
to generate electricity. 
Geothermal Energy - An energy source that harnesses the Earth's natural heat to produce electricity and provide heating 
and cooling 
Biomass Energy - Organic matter like wood, crops, or manure can be burned to produce heat and electricity or converted 
into biofuels for transportation. 
Hydrogen Energy - Renewable sources like biomass and solar power can be used to produce hydrogen gas, which can then 
be stored and used as a clean fuel. 
Tidal and Wave Energy - An energy source that converts the kinetic energy of waves and tides into electricity energy source 
that uses the force of moving water to generate electricity through dams and turbines

how to filter a 
column in 
google sheet
Here's how you can filter a column in Google 
Sheets: 
- Click on the letter at the top of the column you 
want to filter. This selects the entire column. 
- After selecting the column, click on the "Data" 
menu in the top toolbar. From the dropdown 
menu, select "Create a filter." This will add filter 
icons (small funnels) to the header row of your 
selected column. 
- Once the filter is applied, you'll see filter icons in 
the header row of the selected column.  
Click on the filter icon in the header cell (the top 
cell of the column you selected). 
• After clicking the filter icon, a menu will 
appear where you can filter the data in 
various ways: 
• Filter by Values: You can select specific 
values to show or hide. 
• Filter by Condition: You can set conditions 
such as greater than, less than, equal to, etc. 
• After setting your filter criteria, click "OK" or 
"Apply" to apply the filter.
There are many ways to filter a column in Google 
Sheets. Below are examples of some common 
methods. 
Method 1: Filter by Cell Value 
- Select a cell in the column that you want to filter. 
- Click on the "Data" tab in the Google Sheets toolbar. 
- Click on "Filter Views" and select "Create new filter 
view." 
Enter the criteria for the filter in the "Search Criteria" 
field. For example, if you want to filter the column for 
values that are greater than 100, enter ">100" in the 
field. 
Click “Apply." 
Method 2: Filter by Number Format 
• Click on "Filter" and then click on "Number 
Filters." 
• Choose the number format that you want to filter 
by from the drop-down menu. For example, if you 
want to filter the cells for values that are formatted 
as currency, choose "Currency." 
• Click "Apply." 
• Click "Apply."
