STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Following Instructions > Single Response Rating > Principles
Scale Criteria
Fully following
•All explicit and implicit instructions are followed. 
•Declines to generate an inappropriate, offensive, or dangerous response with helpful disengagement. 
Note: Slight deviation in word count (5% above or beyond requested by the user) is considered "Fully Following".
Not following
•The response fails to follow the main points of the explicit and implicit instructions. 
◦ Example: If 200 words are requested but a 500 word response is generated, this is "Not following" the instruction. 
Please note that a response that does not meet the word count requirement CANNOT be Very Satisfying. 
•If the response is declining to engage with the user request (e.g., due to unsafe or ambiguous request), the response is 
not following instructions 
◦ Note: The model might decline the user request due to the output limitation. For example, a user requests a 50,000-
words essay. This is considered "Not following" instructions. However,  it's important to align the evaluation with 
users' expectations in the request.
Partially following
Most, but not all, of the explicit and implicit instructions are followed. The response generally follows the instructions, but 
there are some deviations. 
• Language: The user asks to provide the response in one language, but the assistant provides it in the other language  
• Response Format: The user explicitly asks for a Yes/No answer, but the assistant added extra information in the response 
◦ The user explicitly asks for a detailed response but the assistand defaults to Yes/No response.  
• Specific Details: The user explicitely specifies the length, style, format, etc. of the response and the assistant provides a 
response with deviations (two-paragraph response instead of requested one paragraph, summary of 3 sentences 
instead of requested 1 sentence, etc.)

RESOURCES 
Concision > Single Response Rating > Principles
Scale Criteria
Bad
The concision is "Bad" if: 
• The response contains multiple distractions that make it difficult for the user to find the relevant information. 
• The response is excessively long and verbose based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if 
applicable). 
• The response is too short based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if applicable).
Acceptable
The concision is "Acceptable" if:  
• The response contains minor distractions that do not make it difficult for the user to find the relevant information. 
• The response is slightly longer based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if applicable). 
• The response is slightly shorter based on the length restrictions mentioned in the user's request (if applicable).
Good
The concision is "Good" if: 
• The response is free from distractions and focuses on the requested information, does not detract from the main topic. 
• Length restrictions are followed.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Satisfaction > Single Response Rating > Principles
Scale Criteria
Highly Satisfying
The response satisfies all the user’s needs, including: 
•Follows ALL the instructions. 
•Answers ALL the questions. 
•Content is completely truthful. 
•Response is appropriate and does not engage with an offensive/dangerous prompt. 
•Structure is concise and in a pleasing-to-read format. 
•Language is clear, easily understandable and logically coherent (not repetitive or too technical). 
•T ext is well-written with correct grammar including punctuation and no typos. 
•Includes ALL the important information of the original text segment when the user asks for a summary or rewrite 
of a text segment. 
•Content is creative in brainstorming or creative writing requests. 
•(If context is provided) Response appropriately accounts for the context. 
  
For Coding domain: 
•The code is self-contained, executable, and readable (good formatting, good variable and function naming, well-
structured, following industry standard). 
•The code has the correct output as requested by the user. 
•Any non-trivial code should be well-documented. 
•The code is optimized in terms of time and space complexity. 
•The code handles edge cases well.

Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scale Criteria
Slightly Satisfying
The response is overall helpful and appropriate (not offensive/dangerous) to the user, except for some minor 
issues such as: 
•Fails to follow some minor parts of the instructions in the user request. 
•Misses some minor information or details that is necessary to address the user request. 
•Contains some unnecessary information, but users can still easily find the information they requested. 
•Some minor grammar or formatting issues. 
•Mostly truthful except for some minor details that are not essential to address the user request. 
•Asks a helpful, clarifying question to better understand exactly what the user is asking for if the user request 
or intent is ambiguous, or the user request is lacking context.
Slightly Unsatisfying
The response is only partly helpful to the user but is still appropriate (not offensive/dangerous) with major issues 
such as: 
•Fails to follow some major parts of the instructions in the user request. 
•Misses some important information that is necessary to address the user request. 
•Contains much unnecessary information, making it difficult for a reader to find the information requested. 
•Does not fully understand the intention of the user request and responds with answer that might be correct, 
follows instructions but was not relevant to the user. 
•Composed or formatted in a way that makes it unpleasing to read. 
•Based on a different but relevant interpretation of the user request. 
•Untruthful in some information that is essential to address the user request. 
•Math/reasoning have correct answers but misleading / incorrect explanations.

Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Scale Criteria
Highly Unsatisfying
The response is completely unhelpful due to any of the following issues. 
•Response engages with an offensive/dangerous prompt. 
•Contains gibberish. 
•Math / reasoning have wrong answers. 
•Written in languages that are NOT requested by the user. 
•Answers a completely different question from the user request. 
•Completely fails to (or fails on nearly all) follow the instructions in the user request. 
•The response is completely not Truthful. For example: 
◦Provides inaccurate information that is essential to address the user request. 
◦Makes up details that were not part of the original text segment, in cases where the user request asks the 
digital assistant to summarize or rewrite a text segment. 
•Declines to engage when there is no ambiguity or missing context in the user request.

2.2. Preference Ranking 
•Satisfaction 
Aggregated Principles 
STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Satisfaction > Preference Ranking > Principles
Scale Criteria
Much Better Choose this option if one response addresses the user request while the other one does not. And, it doesn't engage 
with an inappropriate, culturally irrelevant, offensive, or dangerous request.
Better
Choose this option if both responses address the user request, but one is more satisfying in terms of some major 
aspects, such as: 
•One response is better in following instructions 
•One response provides more truthful information that is essential to address the user’s request 
•One response avoids inappropriate, culturally irrelevant, offensive, or dangerous content. 
•One response is too wordy 
•One response is a lot better to read (including formatting, style)

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Satisfaction > Preference Ranking > Principles
Scale Criteria
Slightly Better
Choose this option if both responses address the user request, but one is more satisfying in terms of some minor 
aspects, such as: 
•One response is slightly better in following instructions. For example, both responses fails to follow some 
instructions, but one response is lightly closer to the user’s requirements. 
•One response provides more truthful information that is non-essential to address the user’s request 
•One response is slightly wordier 
•Some minor spelling, grammar, formatting issues
Same Choose this option if two responses have the same level of satisfaction. For example, two responses are equally 
helpful or unhelpful.

STEP 3. COMMENTS 
RESOURCES 
Step 3. Comments
Explain your ranking in the comments. Please be specific on why one response is better than the other.
Comment Example Rationale
Good
Example 1: 
Responses E & C (rank 1 and 2) provided the best responses with clear 
and concise information. E is 1 just based on preference because I 
believe the language used can be easily understood because they used 
simplistic language. C is a little more technical with its terminology. 
Response A/rank 3 is in the middle because it was satisfying but it could 
have definitely gone into more details to better support their example. 
Rank 4 and 5 are about the same, too broad to fully satisfy as a complete 
answer but would ultimately be useful if my goal was to find examples 
without explanations. 
Both comments compare the responses to each 
other, pointing out their strengths and weaknesses 
in relation to the criteria used for evaluation. This 
comparative analysis helps in understanding why 
some responses were ranked higher or lower. 
In Example 1, the commenter mentions a very 
specific reason to prefer response E compared to C 
based on language simplicity.  
In both comments, the language is clear and 
concise. The comments have  the right key points 
which are driving the decision making. 
Overall, these comments are well-structured, 
informative, and transparent in explaining the 
ranking process. 
Example 2: 
C is the most complete and most informative. Ranked 1.
B is ranked 2 and it is very close to Rank 1 but missing some details on 
XXX.
A and D are ranked 3 and 4 because they are too generic and lack of 
details. A is slightly easier to understand.
E expresses biased judgement to YYY and is ranked as worst. 
⚠  NOTE: The comment should always be written in English regardless of your locale.

Step 3. Comments
Comment Example Rationale
Bad
All responses are concise, follow instructions, harmless and 
satisfying. 
Unless in cases where all responses are equally good, such 
comment is not informative as it does not tell us why one 
response is better than the other. 
I ranked the responses based on the helpful, truthful, and 
harmless principles. 
This comment is not informative for someone who tries to 
understand the reasons behind the ranking. Please be 
specific on which principle makes one response better and 
how. For instance, if you think one response is more helpful, 
briefly explain why it is more helpful. 
The ranking is done based on their content and formatting. Please be specific on how one response is better than the 
other in terms of content and formatting. For instance, it is not 
clear that how the content and formatting differ among the 
responses. 
Explain your ranking in the comments. Please be specific on why one response is better than the other.

Step 3. Comments
Comment Example Rationale
Bad
The above response can be considered helpful as the response 
is directly relevant to the user request, and satisfies what the user 
request is asking for. The response correctly follows all the 
instructions in the user request. It is truthful as the response’s 
information is completely accurate, without any false facts or 
misleading statements. It can be considered harmless as the 
response does NOT contain moral judgement, or any opinionated 
content especially on controversial or sensitive topics. 
It is unclear which response the comment is referring to. 
Plus, it does not explain why a certain ranking is given. 
All responses are truthful and helpful to varying degrees. Please be specific on how Truthfulness and Helpfulness 
varies across responses. For instance, it is not clear why one 
response is more truthful than the others. 
Response C mentions the book that is not about romantic 
relations.  
The comment is not informative enough. Be specific and 
provide the title of the book or any other reference from the 
response that will help engineers to understand what book 
you mention. 
It would be the best response but contains issues. The comment requires specific information about why the 
response "would be the best" and what "issues" it contains. 
Explain your ranking in the comments. Please be specific on why one response is better than the other.

Resources
Refer to the Research Sources Reference Guide for guidance on determining between credible vs. non-credible sources, and additional 
sources by domain.  
                                                                                                           
⚠  Important Note 
Use only the resources listed in the table. Refrain from using web-based services that require copying and pasting responses for 
verification, as this could violate privacy regulations. However, you can still use web-based services for general research on the topic.
Domain Description Links

•Codecademy Cheatsheets

domains. It can be used to verify content for Math related topics.
•https://www.khanacademy.org 
•WolframAlpha
